Minipg: PostgreSQL as a Single Executable
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-26
03:00:48 UTC
12:00:48 KST

minipg is PostgreSQL as one executable, ready to use, with no server to configure. A .pgdb directory is the database: its data, config, credentials, and Postgres version all live inside it, and minipg app.pgdb opens a psql shell on it. A server starts when a command needs one and stops on its own when it's idle.
minipg embeds Docker Hub's postgres:18-alpine image and runs it with h9, used as a Zig library. h9's Linux personality runs Alpine's own Postgres binaries directly on the host's hypervisor, with h9's Plan 9 kernel answering their system calls. There's no Linux kernel to boot, no VM image, and no Docker.
The demo downloads about 35 MB. It needs JavaScript Promise Integration, which Chrome and Edge have from version 137; other browsers are getting it. It can also be opened on its own page.
The demo runs minipg's Linux build, which runs inside the Postgres image itself. It starts Postgres as ordinary processes and serves on a unix socket, since a page has no TCP. New databases come from a template cluster made at build time, because initdb takes half a minute on v86.
minipg app.pgdb                     # psql shell (creates app.pgdb if missing)
minipg app.pgdb "select 1"          # one statement
minipg url app.pgdb                 # postgres://postgres:...@127.0.0.1:54321/app
minipg serve app.pgdb               # an explicit foreground server
minipg run app.pgdb -- npm start    # your app, with DATABASE_URL set
minipg dump app.pgdb > dump.sql     # and minipg restore app.pgdb dump.sql
A .pgdb holds minipg.toml (settings and the generated superuser password) and pgdata/, Postgres's data directory as plain files. A stopped one can be copied like any directory. New clusters use Postgres's builtin C.UTF-8 locale, which sorts the same on every platform.
In CI, the Linux binary is static and needs no privileges or devices: without /dev/kvm, h9 runs Postgres on its native backend. A test job can be as small as:
minipg init test.pgdb
minipg run test.pgdb -- go test ./...
The README lists every command, and the manual is the full guide.
Exactly one process runs Postgres for a .pgdb, enforced by a lock. It holds the server's h9 machine, whose Linux root is the unpacked image and whose writable working directory is the .pgdb. The image's docker-entrypoint.sh initializes the cluster on first start. Commands never host Postgres themselves. If no server is running, they start one in the background and use it. psql, pg_dump, and the other tools each run in their own short-lived h9 machine (about 90 ms to start) over the same root.
The source is on GitHub, under the MIT license. Building needs Zig 0.16, plus curl, jq, tar, and xz for the root:
git clone --recursive https://github.com/progrium/minipg
cd minipg
make build         # pulls the root for this host's arch, builds ./minipg
make root ARCH=386
make web           # this page's demo, in .zig-out/web
minipg is early, and h9's Linux personality is young. On h9's native backend, Postgres is about 2x slower than native for one client, and slower under load. Don't trust it with data you can't recreate yet.
Please report bugs and send questions through GitHub issues.

====================================================================================================

minipg run test.pgdb -- go test ./...
The README lists every command, and the manual is the full guide.
Exactly one process runs Postgres for a .pgdb, enforced by a lock. It holds the server's h9 machine, whose Linux root is the unpacked image and whose writable working directory is the .pgdb. The image's docker-entrypoint.sh initializes the cluster on first start. Commands never host Postgres themselves. If no server is running, they start one in the background and use it. psql, pg_dump, and the other tools each run in their own short-lived h9 machine (about 90 ms to start) over the same root.
The source is on GitHub, under the MIT license. Building needs Zig 0.16, plus curl, jq, tar, and xz for the root:
git clone --recursive https://github.com/progrium/minipg
cd minipg
make build         # pulls the root for this host's arch, builds ./minipg
make root ARCH=386
make web           # this page's demo, in .zig-out/web
minipg is early, and h9's Linux personality is young. On h9's native backend, Postgres is about 2x slower than native for one client, and slower under load. Don't trust it with data you can't recreate yet.
Please report bugs and send questions through GitHub issues.

====================================================================================================

Disney Plus, Hulu, and HBO Max are all over 50 percent off for Black Friday
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-11-26
01:29:46 UTC
10:29:46 KST

Black Friday isn’t just a good time to save on a new TV but also a chance to spend less on what you watch on it. The shopping holiday often brings steep streaming discounts, and this year is no exception, with some streamers offering more than 50 percent off. HBO Max, Apple TV, and Hulu are all offering deals, with several plans going for just a few dollars a month. Some discounts even apply to ad-free plans, though there are fewer than last year and prices are generally higher.
Disney Plus, Hulu, and HBO Max are all over 50 percent off for Black Friday
Many services are offering discounts that last anywhere from two months to a full year.
Still, you can save big if you don’t mind a few commercials. Right now, for example, you can get a year of the ad-supported Disney Plus and Hulu bundle for $4.99 a month, saving you $96, or sign up for the most basic HBO Max plan for $5.49 a month for 12 months, saving you $66. We’ve rounded up these deals and more below, including what each plan offers and how long you can take advantage of the discounted rate.
Disney Plus and Hulu
With everything from Disney classics and Marvel movies to FX dramas like The Bear and Shōgun, the Disney Plus and Hulu bundle offers plenty for the whole family to watch. For Black Friday, you can get both services with ads for just $4.99 per month ($8 off) for a year via Hulu or Disney Plus, saving you $96 in total. The ad-free bundle is also on sale for $14.99 per month ($7 off) for 12 months, saving you $84. Both discounts are available through 11:59PM PT on November 30th.
Whichever plan you choose, you’ll get access to both streaming libraries. Hulu offers dramas like Furious, Alien: Earth, The Lowdown, and The Handmaid’s Tale, as well as comedies like Bob’s Burgers, Futurama, and It’s Always Sunny in Philadelphia. Disney Plus, meanwhile, is home to Disney and Pixar favorites, The Simpsons, and a wide selection of Star Wars and Marvel movies and shows. It’s also a good option for holiday movie nights, with classics like Home Alone and The Santa Clause.
HBO Max
HBO Max is home to some of Hollywood’s biggest releases, and right now, you can watch them for half the usual rate. Now through November 30th, new and returning subscribers can sign up for HBO Max’s Basic With Ads plan for $5.49 a month via HBO Max or Amazon, saving you $66 over the course of a year.
Although the plan doesn’t include live sports, it supports simultaneous streaming on two devices and gives you access to everything from award-winning films like Sinners and Marty Supreme to Wuthering Heights. There’s arguably even more to watch on the TV front, from dramas like The Gilded Age, Lanterns, Euphoria, and Heated Rivalry to comedies such as Hacks and The Righteous Gemstones. Fantasy fans, meanwhile, can immerse themselves in the world of Game of Thrones and its spinoffs, including House of the Dragon and A Knight of the Seven Kingdoms, or check out Dune: Prophecy. You’ll also be able to stream new releases, including HBO’s upcoming Harry Potter series.
Peacock
If you’re looking for an affordable way to catch up on NBC shows, Peacock’s Black Friday promotion offers two ways to save. Through November 30th, new and returning subscribers can sign up for Peacock Select for $2.99 a month ($6 off) for three months or $29.99 ($60 off) for a full year. For access to Peacock’s larger catalog, you can also subscribe to Peacock Premium for $8.99 per month for three months or $89.99 ($40 off) for a year.
The cheaper Select tier is a particularly good value if you mainly watch shows from networks like NBC, Bravo, and Telemundo, including Saturday Night Live, The Office, and Below Deck. Movie buffs and sports fans, however, will prefer Premium, which offers access to a much wider selection of entertainment. Peacock’s catalog includes films like The Super Mario Galaxy Movie, Disclosure Day, and Minions & Monsters, along with shows like The Good Daughter, Dig, and The Paper. A Premium subscription also lets you watch live sporting events, including Sunday Night Football, as well as Peacock originals, such as the various Love Island spinoffs.
Paramount Plus
If you’re a Star Trek fan or want another way to watch live sports, Paramount Plus is down to $2.99 per month for two months for Black Friday. New and eligible returning subscribers can take advantage of the discount through November 30th, bringing the cost of both the Essential and Premium plans to $5.98 for the introductory period. That makes Premium a particularly good deal, since it normally costs $13.99 a month, compared to $8.99 for the ad-supported Essential tier.
Paramount Plus includes CBS shows like NCIS and Everybody Loves Raymond to Tulsa King and the various Yellowstone spinoffs, including both 1883 and 1923. Movie fans can also browse Paramount’s film library, which includes titles like Arrival, Top Gun, and Interstellar, while sports fans can watch select NFL games, UFC events, UEFA Champions League matches, and March Madness games. Premium also grants you access to Showtime originals and a live feed of your local CBS station, along with ad-free on-demand viewing, though live programming still includes commercials.
Apple TV
Apple TV has built an impressive collection of acclaimed shows, particularly if you’re into sci-fi shows like Silo or thrillers like Slow Horses. Right now, US customers with an Amazon account can subscribe to Apple TV through Amazon for $6.99 per month ($8 off) for six months, saving $48 over the introductory period. Amazon hasn’t listed an end date for the promotion, either.
Six months should give you plenty of time to work through Apple’s other originals, including Severance, Pachinko, The Morning Show, and Pluribus, or settle in for a movie like Arrival or Anchorman. The service also includes live sports, specifically Formula 1 races and Major League Soccer matches.
Crunchyroll
Anime fans can spend the next six months catching up on shows like The Apothecary Diaries, Jujutsu Kaisen, Black Clover, and One Piece for a lot less thanks to Crunchyroll’s Black Friday sale. Select new and returning subscribers in the US can sign up for the Fan plan for $3.99 a month ($6 off) for six months, saving them $36. The Mega Fan tier is also down to $5.99 a month ($8 off) for six months, while Ultimate Fan is available for $8.99 a month ($9 off) for the same period. Amazon is also offering the same discount on the Mega Fan tier.
All three subscriptions offer access to Crunchyroll’s full anime library without ads, including new episodes shortly after they air in Japan. The Fan tier lets you watch on one device at a time, while Mega Fan increases that limit to four devices and adds offline viewing and access to Crunchyroll’s Game Vault. Ultimate Fan, meanwhile, supports six simultaneous streams and includes Crunchyroll Manga, along with additional store perks.
Starz
If you didn’t get a chance to watch the Michael Jackson biopic Michael when it was released earlier this year or just want to catch up on Outlander, Starz is offering a particularly affordable way to stream both on Black Friday. Eligible subscribers can sign up for a year directly through Starz or Amazon Prime for $11.99 ($58 off), which works out to about $1 a month when you pay upfront. If you’d rather not commit to a year, you can alternatively subscribe for $2.99 per month ($9 off) for three months. Either option lets you explore Starz’s catalog of films and TV shows, including In the Grey, Power Ballad, Splash City, and P-Valley.
Other streaming deals of note
Amazon is also discounting a wide range of subscriptions through Prime Video, including NBA League Pass, AMC Plus, MGM Plus, and several bundles. Most of the deals below are available through November 30th.
- Now through December 1st, you can get the standard version of NBA League Pass for $49.99 ($60 off); the Premium tier is also available for $74.99 ($85 off).
- You can subscribe to AMC Plus Premium for $2.75 per month (about $8 off) for two months through November 30th, or get the ad-free plan for $1.99 per month ($8 off) for two months.
- MGM Plus is down to $1.99 ($6 off) per month for two months. If you’d like both services, the AMC Plus Premium and MGM Plus bundle is available for $3.49 per month ($10 off) for two months through November 30th. You can also get AMC Plus, MGM Plus, and Starz together for $5.49 per month (about $16 off) for two months.
- Horror fans can also bundle MGM Plus and horror service Shudder through Prime Video for $12.99 a month ($5 off), down from the usual combined price of $17.98 a month.
- If you’re planning a holiday movie marathon this weekend, Hallmark Plus is available for $2.25 per month (about $7 off) for three months.
- You can subscribe to PBS Kids for $1.25 per month (about $4 off) for two months.
- BritBox, which offers British series like The Other Bennet Sister, is available for $2.75 per month (around $8 off) for two months.
